# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

} // namespace functions<|end|><|start|>user<|message|>Instructions: Answer the following question about drug properties.

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

   - MW: **~300 Da** (below 400 Da, favorable).  
   - logP (Wildman–Crippen): **1.5** – notably lower than the typical 2–4 range for CNS drugs.  
   - TPSA: **≈42 Å²** – low and favorable for permeability.  
   - HBD: 1; HBA: 4 – within acceptable limits.  
   - Rotatable bonds: 1 – very rigid.

2. **Ionisation and logD** (from `assess_adme_properties`)  
   - Most basic pKa ≈ 8.0 → significant protonation at pH 7.4.  
   - logD(7.4) = **0.79** – much below the ~1.5–3.0 window that most BBB‑permeable drugs exhibit.

3. **Structural features** (from `analyze_ring_systems`)  
   - 5‑ring scaffold, only one aromatic ring; rigid, but lack of additional lipophilic surface.  
   - Contains a tertiary amine (basic) and a single hydroxyl – polar functionalities that can reduce passive permeability.

4. **BBB penetration rules of thumb**  
   - logP > 2 AND logD > 1 AND TPSA < 90 Å² are good predictors.  
   - Our molecule satisfies TPSA and MW but fails on logP/logD, indicating poor passive diffusion.

5. **Conclusion**  
   - The low lipophilicity (logD 0.79) and presence of a protonated amine strongly suggest the compound would have limited ability to cross the blood‑brain barrier.

Therefore, the prediction is **(A) does not cross the BBB**.<|return|>

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

} // namespace functions<|end|><|start|>user<|message|>Instructions: Answer the following question about drug properties.

**Reasoning**

1. **Physicochemical profile**  
   - **MW**: 161 Da – well below the 400 Da threshold.  
   - **logP (Wildman‑Crippen)**: 2.13 – within the optimal lipophilicity range (1‑3) for passive BBB diffusion.  
   - **TPSA**: 26 Å² – far below the 90 Å² ceiling that limits permeability.  
   - **H-bond donors/acceptors**: 1 each – minimal polar functional groups.

2. **Ionization state**  
   - **pKa** of the primary amine ≈ 9.5.  
   - At physiological pH (7.4) the molecule is largely protonated (~99 %), yet the small size and high lipophilicity of the neutral form allow significant passive diffusion.  Drugs with similar structures (e.g., amphetamine) are known to cross the BBB despite a primary amine.

3. **Structure‑activity expectations**  
   - The molecule possesses a simple phenethyl‑like scaffold with only one aliphatic amine.  
   - No heavy atom clusters, PAH or macrocycles that would hinder permeability.  
   - The absence of additional polar or bulky substituents keeps the complexity low.

4. **Comparative precedent**  
   - Amphetamine (C9H13N, logP ≈ 2.0, pKa ≈ 9.4) is a classic example of a small, basic compound that efficiently penetrates the BBB.  
   - Our compound is even smaller, has a similar logP, and only one extra unsaturated bond that does not materially alter its lipophilicity.

Given these points, the compound is predicted to **penetrate** the blood‑brain barrier.<|return|>

# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

} // namespace functions<|end|><|start|>user<|message|>Instructions: Answer the following question about drug properties.

# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

} // namespace functions<|end|><|start|>user<|message|>Instructions: Answer the following question about drug properties.
Context: Humans are exposed to a variety of chemicals through food, household products, and medicines, some of which can be toxic, leading to over 30% of promising pharmaceuticals failing in human trials due to toxicity. // Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).

}) => any;

smiles: string
}) => any;

type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string

Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.

// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

} // namespace functions<|end|><|start|>user<|message|>Instructions: Answer the following question about drug properties.

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;
